## Shared source texts

### T01cddcd34f85a1d06622ee99

Text hash c5297e77d1d2ad7abe847517b407e782b23dd7ba74e380dfb7ce6b94c3a70e1c; absolute characters 10185–10316; files: jobs/source-ladder/output/texts/queue_EO_10140_Actual_report_date_of_the_board_created_by_EO_10140_Presidential_Emergency_Board_Rail_3199c74bde.txt

```text
The board sh'lll repor~ its findings to the President with respect to the said 
dispute within 30 days from the date of this order.
```

### T0244c172618c254470d755ae

Text hash e44e1dd1757d3364919249800b9fd5337bc3d9b6f1cc51178483eeb2eba81277; absolute characters 0–497; files: jobs/source-ladder/output/texts/queue_EO_10112_Actual_report_date_of_the_board_created_by_EO_10112_Presidential_Emergency_Board_Rail_f4b72f4da4.txt

```text
Wanted: queue:EO:10112:Actual report date of the board created by EO 10112 (Presidential Emergency Board (Railway Labor Act sec. 10): the Carriers Represented by the Eastern Carriers' Conference Committee, the Western Carriers’ Conference Committee, and the Southeastern Carriers' Conference Committee, and Certain of Their Employees): the board's report to the President and its transmittal letter date; not the order's report deadline, and the 1986 revocation by EO 12553 is only an upper bound.
```

### T02d5cf41d4ce538acb0384cf

Text hash de6b4aa9d0dc0ba2ea2396cd5ab7b3aeefb038d7798e6fa16257ed3365915073; absolute characters 6841–7285; files: jobs/clause-drafts/output/texts/cd-job-source-ladder-10032-e2dcf1bbbc.txt

```text
In designating its members, the President instructed the Board 
to "investigate promptly the facts as to such dispute and, on the 
basis of the facts developed, make every effort to adjust the dis- 
pute and report thereon to me within 30 days from the date of the 
Executive order." The Board has given careful consideration to 
the possibility of seeking an adjustment of this dispute through a 
mediated settlement acceptable to the parties.
```

### T09eb07059205fedfca2428f1

Text hash de6b4aa9d0dc0ba2ea2396cd5ab7b3aeefb038d7798e6fa16257ed3365915073; absolute characters 11026–11240; files: jobs/clause-drafts/output/texts/cd-job-source-ladder-10032-e2dcf1bbbc.txt

```text
The issue before us, then, is closely related to the report of the 
1943 Diesel Board and especially to the agreements made be- 
tween the organization and the carriers subsequent to the report 
made by that Board.
```

```text
Washington, Friday, July 22, 1949
TITLE 3— THE PRESIDENT
EXECUTIVE ORDER 10070
Amending E xecutive  O rder N o. 9975 of 
July  7,1948, P rescribing R egulations 
Governing the A llowance of T ravel 
Expenses  of C laimants and B ene ­
ficiaries of the Veterans’  Administra ­
tion and T heir A ttendants
By virtue of and pursuant to the au­
thority vested in me by section 1 of the  
act of March 14, 1940, 54 Stat. 49, as  
amended by the act of June 16, 1948, 62  
Stat. 471 (38 U. S. C. 76), section 2 of  
Executive Order No. 9975 of July 7, 1948,  
entitled “Regulations Governing the Al­
lowance of Travel Expenses of Claimants  
and Beneficiaries of the Veterans’  Ad­
ministration and Their Attendants,” is  
hereby amended to read as follows:
“2. The Administrator of Veterans’  
Affairs may authorize in lieu of actual  
expenses of travel, including lodging and  
subsistence, payment of an allowance of  
5 cents a mile to any claimant or bene­
ficiary of the Veterans’  Administration  
traveling under prior authorization to or  
from a Veterans’  Administration facility, 
or other place, in connection with vo­
cational rehabilitation or for the pur­
pose of examination, treatment, or care,  
or, in his discretion, to the person who  
or the organization which has actually  
paid the expenses of such travel, includ­
ing lodging and subsistence: Provided,  
that payment of mileage in connection  
with vocational rehabilitation or upon  
termination of examination, treatment,  
or care may be made prior to completion  
of such travel.”
This order shall become effective on  
August 1,1949.
Harry S. T ruman 
The W hite  H ouse ,
July 20, 1949.
IP. R. Doc. 49-6065; Piled, July 21, 1949;
10:36 a. m.]
EXECUTIVE ORDER 10071
Creating an E mergency O rder to I nves ­
tigate a D ispute  B etween  the S outh ­
ern P acific C ompany (P acific L ines ) 
and C ertain of its  E mployees
WHEREAS a dispute exists between  
the Southern Pacific Company (Pacific  
Lines), a carrier, and certain of its em­
ployees represented by the Brotherhood
of Railroad Trainmen, a labor organiza­
tion; and
WHEREAS this dispute has not here­
tofore been adjusted under the provisions  
of the Railway Labor Act, as amended;  
and
WHEREAS this dispute, in the judg­
ment of the National Mediation Board,  
threatens substantially to interrupt in­
terstate commerce to a degree such as  
to deprive a large section of the country  
of essential transportation service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the  
authority vested in me by section 10 of  
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45  
U. S. C. 160), I hereby create a board of  
three members, to be appointed by me, to  
investigate the said dispute. No mem­
ber of the said board shall be pecuniarily  
or otherwise interested in any organiza­
tion of railway employees or any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to  
the President with respect to the said  
dispute within thirty days from the date  
of this order.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this  
date and for thirty days after the board  
has made its report to the President, no  
change, except by agreement, shall be  
made by the Southern Pacific Company  
(Pacific Lines) or its employees in the  
conditions out of which the said dispute  
arose.
H arry S. T ruman
T he W hite  H ouse ,
July 20, 1949.
[F. R. Doc. 49-6066; Filed, July 21, 1949;  
10:36 a. m.]
```

### T0e3398b4aedc688297737df6

Text hash 4574a3014cd5e2858373e00bcea533c0b6c25f8a3f5bf38e7ebdd127c94013d4; absolute characters 7815–8279; files: jobs/source-ladder/output/texts/queue_EO_10757_Actual_report_date_of_the_board_created_by_EO_10757_Presidential_Emergency_Board_Rail_22203830f5.txt

```text
BACKGROUND OFTHIS DISPUTE 
It is necessary to state in this regard only that the contracts be- 
tweeff Districts 100, 142, 143, 144, and 145, and Lodge 1726 of the 
,International Association of Machinists on the one hand, and the 
respective six Carriers here involved on the other, expired either on 
September 30, 1957, or October 1~ 1957, but that the negotiations for 
new contracts reached impasses which resulted in the Executive Order 
creating this Board.
```

```text
THE PRESIDENT,
```

### T1e9d83d9c119445dcbba94ca

```text
In designating its members, the President instructed the Board 
to "investigate promptly the facts as to such dispute and, on the 
basis of the facts developed, make every effort to adjust the dis- 
pute and report thereon to me within 30 days from the date of the 
Executive order." The Board has given careful consideration to 
the possibility of seeking an adjustment of this dispute through a 
mediated settlement acceptable to the parties.
```

```text
THE PRESIDENT,

MR. PRESIDENT: The Emergency Board appointed by you on 
February 24, 1950, pursuant to section 10 of the Railway Labor 
Act, to investigate a rules controversy involving substantially all 
of the Nation's railroads and certain of their employees, repre­
sented by the Order of Railway Conductors and the Brotherhood

in dispute.
```

```text
Presidential Documents
Title 3— THE PRESIDENT
Executive Order 10872
CREATING AN EMERGENCY BOARD  
TO INVESTIGATE A DISPUTE BE-  
TWEEN T H E  P A N  AMERICAN 
WORLD AIRWAYS, INC., A N D

WHEREAS a dispute exists between  
the Pan American World Airways, Inc.,  
a carrier, and certain of its employees  
represented by the Brotherhood of Rail­
way and Steamship Clerks, Freight Han­
dlers, Express and Station Employes, a  
labor organization; and
WHEREAS this dispute has not here­
tofore been adjusted under the provisions  
of the Railway Labor Act, as amended;  
and
WHEREAS this dispute, in the judg­
ment of the National Mediation Board,  
threatens substantially to interrupt in­
terstate commerce to a degree such as  
to deprive a section of the country of  
essential transportation service:
NOW,. THEREFORE, by virtue of the  
authority vested in me by section 10 of  
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45  
U.S.C. 160), I hereby create a board of  
three members, to be appointed by me,  
to investigate this dispute. No member  
of the board shall be pecuniarily or  
otherwise interested in any organization  
of airline employees or any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to  
the President with respect to the dispute  
within thirty days from the date of this  
order.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this  
date and for thirty days after the board  
has made its report to the President, no  
change, except by agreement, shall be  
made by the Pan American World Air­
ways, Inc., or by its employees, in the  
conditions out of which the dispute arose.
```

```text
9  GENERAL
§  648.71 Applicability of  §§648.71 to 
648.85.' Sections 648.71 to 648.85 shall 
govern the establishment of farm acre­
age goals for Irish potatoes for use in 
connection with United States Depart­
ment of Agriculture price support opera­
tions relating to the 1949 crop of such 
potatoes. The planting of potatoes in ex­
cess of the 1949 goal established for any 
farm, or, where separate goals are estab­
lished for early and late potatoes, the 
planting or potatoes in excess of either 
of such goals, shall, subsequent to the 
date of such excess planting, render any 
person having an interest in such farm
as operator, owner, landlord, tenant or 
partner ineligible to participate in 1949 
potato price support operations. Such 
ineligibility shall extend also to any cor­
poration or corporate stockholder whose 
operations are subject to substantially 
the same management, ownership, or 
control as those of a corporation or cor­
porate stockholder planting potatoes in 
excess of an acreage goal. Potatoes 
which are produced on a farm owned or 
operated by a partnership shall be in­
eligible in case any partner plants pota­
toes on any other farm in excess of the 
acreage goal for such farm. The entire 
interest in potatoes planted within a 
non-commercial farm goal must be in the 
owner, or in the owner and the operator, 
of such entire farm. Any person having 
an interest, other than as owner or opera­
tor of the entire farm, in potato produc­
tion from one or more non-commercial 
farms, shall be ineligible to participate 
in 1949 potato price support operations. 
Publicly owned experiment stations 
planting potatoes for experimental pur­
poses only do not come within the scope 
of this program; no goal shall be estab­
lished for such experimental plantings 
nor shall the potatoes produced thereon 
be eligible for acquisition under price 
support operations.
§  648.72 Definitions. As used in 
§§ 648.71 to 648.85 and in all instructions, 
forms, and documents in connection 
therewith, the words and phrases defined 
in this section shall have the meanings 
herein assigned to them unless the con­
text or subject matter requires otherwise.
(a) Committees: (1) “County com­
mittee” means the group of persons 
elected within a county to assist in the 
administration of the Agricultural Con­
servation Program in such county.
(2) “State committee” means the 
group of persons designated as the State 
committee of the Production and Market­
ing Administration charged with the re­
sponsibility of administering Production 
and Marketing Administration programs 
within the State.
(b) “Farm” means ah' adjacent or 
nearby farm land under the same owner­
ship which is operated by one person, 
including also: (1) Any other adjacent 
or nearby farm land which the county 
committee, in accordance with instruc­
tions issued by the Production and Mar­
keting Administration, determines is op­
erated by the same persons as part of 
the same unit with respect to rotation of 
crops and with workstock, farm machin­
ery and labor substantially separate from 
that for any other lands;* and (2) any 
field-rented tract (whether operated by
the same or another person) which, to­
gether with any other land included in 
the farm, constitutes a unit with respect 
to the rotation of crops. A farm shall 
be regarded as located in the county in 
which the principal dwelling is situated, 
or if there is no dwelling thereon it shall 
be regarded as located in the county in 
which the major portion of the farm is 
located.
(c) “New farm” means a farm on 
which potatoes have not been planted 
since 1945 and on which the 1949 farm 
operator’ s personal history of potato pro­
duction since 1945 is not used in deter­
mining the farm goal.
(d) “Old farm” means a farm on 
which potatoes have been planted in one 
or more years since 1945 or on which the 
1949 farm operator’ s personal history of 
potato production since 1945 is used in 
determining the farm goal.
(e) “Commercial farm” means a farm 
for which a preliminary 1949 goal of 
three or more acres is established.
(f) “Non-commercial farm” meçms a 
farm on which potatoes are planted in 
1949 and for which a preliminary 1949 
potato acreage goal of three or more 
acres is not established.
(g) “National goal” means the total 
number of acres of Irish potatoes estab­
lished by the United States Department 
of Agriculture as necessary to produce a 
1949 crop sufficient for all domestic and 
export requirements without unduly cur­
tailing production in any area.
(h) “State goal” means the number of 
acres out of the National goal allocated 
by the Production and Marketing Ad­
ministration to be planted within a par­
ticular State.
(i) “Commercial goal” means that part 
of the total goal which is made available 
for apportioning to commercial farms.
(j) “Non-commercial goal” means that 
part of the total goal which is attributed 
to non-commercial farms. No direct ap­
portionment of the non-commercial goal 
to any farm is provided.
(k) “Preliminary goal” is the acreage 
which the county committee preliminar­
ily determines to be the proper goal for a 
farm, in view of the county limit as here­
inafter defined, past goals on the farm, 
topography, crop rotation practice, and 
land, labor, and equipment available for 
the production, handling, and market­
ing of potatoes grown on the farm.
(l) “Final goal” for a farm Is its pre­
liminary goal after such goal has been 
adjusted by the county committee so that 
the total of all final goals in the county 
shall not exceed the county limit, pur­
suant to the procedure specified in 
§§ 648.73 and 648.78.
```

```text
Operating and Financial History of the Carrier 
The Carrier has experienced a steady decline in both passenger and 
freight traffic since the end of World War II, due to the competition 
of alternative modes of transportation. [['his trend has occurred in 
the face of a 35.7-percent increase in the population on Long Island 
since 1946.
```

```text
WHEREAS a dispute exists between 
the Pullman Company, a carrier, and 
certain of its employees represented by 
the Order of Railway Conductors, a labor 
organization; and
WHEREAS this dispute has not here­
tofore been adjusted under the provi­
sions of the Railway Labor Act, as 
amended; and
WHEREAS this dispute, in the judg­
ment of the National Mediation Board, 
threatens substantially to interrupt in­
terstate commerce to a degree such as to 
deprive the country of essential trans­
portation service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the 
authority vested in me by section 10 of 
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 
U. S. C. 160), I hereby create a board of 
three members, to be appointed by me, 
to investigate the said dispute. No mem­
ber of the said board shall be pecuniarily 
or otherwise interested in any organi­
zation of railway employees or any 
carrier.
The board shall report its findings to 
the President with respect to the said 
dispute within thirty days from the date 
of this order.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this 
date and for thirty days after the board 
has made its report to the President, no 
change, except by agreement, shall be 
made by the Pullman Company or its 
employees in the conditions out of which 
the said dispute arose.
H arry S. T ruman
The W hite  H ouse ,
July 6, 1950.
[F. R. Doc. 50-5953; Filed, July 6, 1950;  
2:05 p. m.]

keting Administration, United States

which in turn will make advances to 
eligible producers either directly or 
through auction warehouses. Loans
```

```text
(2) 12-year rehabilitation program was outlined which was estimated 
to cost $65,613,000; (3) during the 12-year redevelopment period the 
Carrier was given the right to charge fares sufficient to meet operat- 
ing expenses plus the cost of the improvement program ; (4) during 
the first 9 years of the plan, all nonreal estate State and local taxes 
(which previously amounted to $500,000 annually) were eliminated 
and real estate taxes were stabilized at less than $1,800,000 annually 
as compared with a previous level in excess of $3,500,000.
```

```text
In order to make possible a complete investigation of the dis- 
pute, the Board found it necessary to request of the President, 
by letter dated February 14, 1949, an extension of 45 days from 
February 27 as the time within which its repor~ was to be made~ 
The parties to the proceeding stipulated their approval of this 
request. 1 Acting upon the recommendation of the National Medi- 
ation Board, the President on February 19, 1949, approved the 
extension request "permitting this Emergency Board to file its re- 
port and recommendations not later than April 13, 1949. ''2 
The hearings of the Board were held from February 7, 1949, 
to February 24, 1949, (inclusive) and from March 14, 1949, to 
March 23, 1949, (inclusive).
```

```text
TITLE 8— -A LIE N S  A N D 
N A T IO N A L IT Y
Chapter 1 — Immigration and Natural­
ization Service, Department of  
Justice
P art  235a— P r e e x a m in a tio n of  A l ie n s
W ithin  the U nited S tates
PREEXAMINATION ELIGIBILITY
The following amendment to Chapter 
I of Title 8 of the Code of Federal Reg­
ulations is hereby prescribed:
The first sentence of § 235a.l Applica­
tion  is amended to read as follows: “Any 
alien, except a citizen of Canada, Mexico, 
or islands adjacent to the United States, 
shall apply for preexamination on Form 
1-63 if he intends to apply to a consu­
lar officer of the United States in Canada 
for an immigrant visa and he believes 
that he will be admissible to the United 
States under all the provisions of the 
immigration laws if in possession of an 
immigrant visa, or that he is prima facie 
eligible for a waiver of excludability un­
der section 5 or 7 of the act of September 
11, 1957; that he will be able to obtain 
the prompt issuance of an immigrant 
visa, and that he is a person of good 
moral character.”
(Sec. 103, 66 Stat. 173; 8 U. S. C. 1103)
This order shall become effective on 
the date of its publication in the Federal 
R egister. Compliance with the provi­
sions of section 4 of the Administrative 
Procedure Act (60 Stat. 238; 5 U. S. C. 
1003) as to notice of proposed rule mak­
ing and delayed effective date is unnec­
essary in this instance because the rule 
prescribed by the order relieves a re­
striction and is clearly advantageous to 
persons affected thereby.
Dated: February 26,1958.
J. M. S w in g , 
Comissioner of
Immigration and Naturalization.

8:49 a. m.]
```

```text
§  953.426 Lemon Regulation 319 —(a) 
Findings. (1) Pursuant to the market­
ing agreement, as amended, and Order 
No. 53, as amended (7 CFR, Cum. Supp., 
953.1 et seq.; 13 F. R. 766), regulating 
the handling of lemons grown in the 
State of California or in the State of 
Arizona, effective under the applicable 
provisions of the Agricultural Marketing 
Agreement Act of 1937, as amended, and 
upon the basis of the recommendation 
and information submitted by the Lemon 
Administrative Committee, established 
under the said amended marketing 
agreement and order, and upon other 
available information, it is hereby found 
that the limitation of the quantity of 
such lemons which may be handled, as 
hereinafter provided, will tend to effec­
tuate the declared policy of the act.
(2) It is hereby further found that it 
is impracticable and contrary to the 
public interest to give preliminary notice, 
engage in public rulemaking procedure, 
and postpone the effective date of this 
section until 30 days after publication 
thereof in the F ederal R egister  (60 Stat. 
237; 5 U. S. C. 1001 et seq.) because the 
time intervening between the date when 
information upon which this section Is 
based became available and the time 
when this section must become effective 
in order to effectuate the declared policy 
of the Agricultural Marketing Agreement 
Act of 1937, as amended, is insufficient, 
and a reasonable time is permitted, under 
the circumstances, for preparation for 
such effective date.
(b) Order. (1) The quantity of lem­
ons grown in the State of California or 
in the State of Arizona which may be 
handled during the period beginning at
```

```text
Since the capacity of the hearing room was approximately 100, the 
Board took the position that it would not proceed until the number 
in attendance was reduced to the figure indicated in its te]egram of 
the night before.
```

```text
/ S , \
REGISTER
VOLUME 15 1 9 3 4  c f r* ^A/iTEO ^ NUMBER 39
Washington, Tuesday, February 28, 1950
TITLE 3— THE PRESIDENT
EXECUTIVE ORDER 10112
Creating an E mergency B oard T o I nves­
tigate a D ispute  Between  the Carriers 
R epresented by the E astern C arriers' 
Conference C ommittee, the W estern 
Carriers’ C onference C ommittee, and 
the S outheastern  C arriers' C onfer­
ence C ommittee, and C ertain of T heir 
Employees
WHEREAS a dispute exists between 
the carriers represented by the Eastern 
Carriers’  Conference Committee, the 
Western Carriers’  Conference Commit­
tee, and the Southeastern Carriers’  Con­
ference Committee, and certain of their 
employees represented by the Order of 
Railway Conductors and the Brother­
hood of Railroad Trainmen, labor or­
ganizations; and
WHEREAS this dispute has not here­
tofore been adjusted under the provi­
sions of the Railway Labor Act, as 
amended; and
WHEREAS this dispute, in the judg­
ment of the National Mediation Board, 
threatens substantially to interrupt in­
terstate commerce to a degree such as to 
' deprive the country of essential trans­
portation service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the 
authority vested in me by section 10 of 
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 
U. S. C. 160), I hereby create a board of 
three members, to be appointed by me, 
to investigate the said dispute. No 
member of the said board shall be pecu­
niarily or otherwise interested in any 
organization of railway employees or 
any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to 
the President with respect to the said 
dispute within thirty days from the date 
of this order.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this 
date add for thirty days after the Board 
has made its report to the President, no 
change, except by agreement, shall be 
made by the carriers represented by the
Eastern Carriers’  Conference Commit­
tee, the Western Carriers’ Conference 
Committee, or the Southeastern Car­
riers’  Conference Committee, or their 
employees in the conditions out of which 
the said dispute arose.
H arry S. T ruman 
The W hite  H ouse,
February 24, 1950.
[F. R. Doc. 60-1653; Filed, Feb. 24, 1950;  
2:43 p. m.]

ident To P rescribe C lothing A llow ­
ances, and C ash A llowances in  L ieu

Armed F orces
By virtue of the authority vested in me 
as President of the United States and as 
Commander in Chief of the armed forces, 
it is ordered as follows:
1. The Secretary of Defense with re­
spect to enlisted men of the Army, the 
Navy, the Air Force, the Marine Corps, 
the Naval Reserve, the Marine Corps Re­
serve, the National Guard, the Air Na­
tional Guard, the National Guard of the 
United States, the Air National Guard of 
the United States, the Organized Reserve 
Corps, and the Air Force Reserve, and the 
Secretary of the Treasury with respect 
to enlisted men of the Coast Guard and 
the Coast Guard Reserve, are hereby 
authorized and directed, after appropri­
ate consultation with the Director of the 
Bureau of the Budget, to perform the 
functions vested in the President by sec­
tion 505 of the Career Compensation Act 
of 1949, approved October 12,1949 (Pub­
lic Law 351, 81st Congress), relative to 
prescribing the quantity and kind of 
clothing which shall be furnished annu­
ally to enlisted men of the aforesaid serv­
ices and relative to prescribing the 
amount of the cash allowance to be paid
```

```text
Presidential Documents
Title 3— THE PRESIDENT
Executive Order 10874
CREATING AN EMERGENCY BOARD
TO INVESTIGATE A DISPUTE BE­
TWEEN THE LONG ISLAND RAIL­
ROAD COMPANY AND CERTAIN OF
ITS EMPLOYEES
WHEREAS a dispute exists between 
The Long Island Railroad Company, a 
carrier, and certain of its employees 
represented by the Brotherhood of Rail­
road Trainmen, a labor organization; 
and
WHEREAS this dispute has not here­
tofore been adjusted under the pro­
visions of the Railway Labor Act, as 
amended; and
WHEREAS this dispute, in the judg­
ment of the National Mediation Board, 
threatens substantially to interrupt in­
terstate commerce to a degree such as to 
deprive a section of the country of 
essential transportation service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the 
authority vested in me by section 10 of 
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45
U.S.C. 160), I  hereby create a board of 
three members, to be appointed by me, 
to investigate this dispute. No member 
of the board shall be pecuniarily or 
otherwise interested in any organization 
of railroad employees or any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to 
the President with respect to the dispute 
within thirty days from the date of this 
order.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this 
date and for thirty days after the board 
has made its report to the President, no 
change, except by agreement, shall be 
made by The Long Island Railroad Com­
pany, or by its employees, in the con­
ditions out of which the dispute arose.
D w ig h t D . Eis e n h o w e r
T h e W h it e H ouse ,
April 18,1960.
[F.R. Doc. 60-3667; Filed, Apr. 19, 1960;
3:21 p.m.]
```

```text
June 2, 1960. 
THE PRESIDENT 
The White House 
Washington, D.G. 
Mr. PRESIDENT : The Emergency Board created by you on March 18, 
1960, by Executive Order 10872, pursuant to Section 10 of the Rail- 
way Labor Act, as amended, to investigate an unadjusted dispute be- 
tween Pan American World Airways, Inc., u Carrier, and certain of

ship Clerks, Freight Handlers, Express and Station Employees, u
```

{"factual_event_ids":[],"factual_obligations":[{"application":{"answer_id":"NFA:astra-pullman-10032-transmittal-v1","checks":{},"clause_evidence":{"char_end":2224,"char_start":580,"id":"CLAUSES:EO:10032#whole","instrument_id":"EO:10032","kind":"whole","label":"sole paragraph","pdf_page":1,"scope_id":"EO:10032#whole","source":{"filing_date":null,"location_confidence":"high","location_verified":false,"page_or_item":"14 FR 429, PDF pages 1-3","requires_page_check":false,"sha256":"d932782219e3f18165b8b34a840324970cee8be32ab592cffd5aa05774209e6d","signing_date":"1949-01-28","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10032.txt","text_sha256":"49c4d157b85005a24041379993faf400dc6ee9078f9f3d5f72f3e7fab7b2b3f6","url":"https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-1949-02-01/pdf/FR-1949-02-01.pdf"},"text":"EXECUTIVE ORDER 10032\nCreating an E mergency B oard to I nves­\ntigate a D ispute  B etween  the A kron, \nCanton &  Youngstown  R ailroad C om­\npany  and O ther C arriers, and C ertain \nof T heir E mployees\nWHEREAS a dispute exists between \nthe Akron, Canton ty  Youngstown Rail­\nroad Company and certain other carriers \ndesignated in the list attached hereto \nand made a part hereof, and certain of \ntheir employees represented by the \nBrotherhood of Locomotive Engineers, a \nlabor organization; and \nWHEREAS this dispute has not here-' \ntofore been adjusted under the provi­\nsions of the Railway Labor Act, as \namended; and\nWHEREAS this dispute, in the judg­\nement of the National Mediation Board, \nthreatens substantially* to interrupt in­\nterstate commerce to a degree such as \nto deprive the country of essential trans­\nportation service:\nNOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the \nauthority vested in me by section 10 of \nthe Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 \nU. S. C. 160), I hereby create a board \nof three members, to be appointed by \nme, to investigate the said dispute. No \nmember of the said board shall be pe­\ncuniarily or otherwise interested in any \norganization of employees or any carrier.\nThe board shall report its findings to \nthe President with respect to the said \ndispute within thirty days from the date of this order.\nAs provided by section 10 of the Rail­\nway Labor Act, as amended, from this \ndate and for thirty days after the board \nhas made its report to the President, no \nchange, except by agreement, shall be \nmade by any of the carriers involved or \nits employees in the conditions out of \nwhich the said dispute arose."},"id":"NA:astra-pullman-10032-transmittal-v1","instrument_id":"EO:10032","legal_effect":"unresolved","producer":"astra-report-bridge-producer","question_key":"NQ:96aecd6e2765dd44cd79b047e3f073d0b5b1e4acc79c578b2a37e19a2f110a7e","schema":"norton-application/v1","scope_id":"EO:10032#whole","state":"candidate"},"application_review":null,"candidate_evidence_ids":[],"citable_factual_events":[],"factual_event_errors":[],"factual_review_status":"candidate","input_origin":"jobs/norton/output/FRAGMENTS.jsonl","legal_effect":"unresolved","machine_lead":true,"question":{"edition_required":"PEB-068 report historical transmittal letter (NMB-held scan)","entity_id":"Presidential Emergency Board 68 established by EO:10032 on 1949-01-28","event_kind":"other_event","interval_end":"1986-02-25","interval_start":"1949-01-28","object":"PEB 68 final report transmittal letter","origin":"owner_seeded","origin_reference":"Owner leverage-first campaign, 2026-10-22; report-deadline ruling 2026-10-21","proposition":"What date does the identified report transmittal letter bear? Inventory: one whole-order clause, the dossier span at offsets 580 to 2224 of the held text, from the order's heading through its last operative sentence (the sentence barring changes in conditions). Text: The order recites that a dispute exists between the Akron, Canton & Youngstown Railroad Company and certain other carriers designated in a list attached to the order, and certain of their employees represented by the Brotherhood of Locomotive Engineers, a labor organization. Acting under section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 U.S.C. 160), it creates a board of three members, to be appointed by the President, to investigate the dispute; it bars any board member from being pecuniarily or otherwise interested in any organization of employees or any carrier; it directs the board to report its findings to the President within thirty days from the date of the order; and, as provided by section 10, it bars any of the carriers involved or its employees from changing, except by agreement, the conditions out of which the dispute arose, from the date of the order until thirty days after the board has made its report to the President. The list of carriers (Eastern, Western and Southeastern regions) follows the signature block in the held text; it identifies the carriers and is not a separate command. Ending: Executive Order 12553 of February 25, 1986 (51 FR 7237, held govinfo text layer) has an enacting sentence reading, in part, \"it is hereby ordered that the following Executive Orders, and\" and, on the next line, \"any amendments thereto, are revoked:\" (the table row for 10032 describes creating an emergency board to investigate a railroad labor dispute). The enacting sentence has no effective-date wording and no exception or saving clause, so the first end is dated on signing, 1986-02-25 (a reading disclosed for the reviewer; the March 3, 1986 printing date is not used). Report: the held National Mediation Board copy of the board's report (jobs/source-ladder/output/texts/queue_EO_10032_Actual_report_date_of_the_board_created_by_EO_10032_Presidential_Emergency_Board_Rail_cf5124cbbf.txt, a held text lead and not an authenticated original) has a cover page dated April 11, 1949 and a letter of transmittal to the President dated Washington, D.C., April 11, 1949; the board states that the President approved, on February 19, 1949, an extension permitting it to file its report not later than April 13, 1949. The report deadline and the section 10 standstill are spent facts and not ends under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 (option C): taking April 11, 1949 as the date the board made its report, the order was functionally spent on April 11, 1949 and the thirty-day period after the report ran to May 11, 1949 (a computed date, thirty days after that cover or transmittal date, not a date shown in a source); the order stayed on the books until the express revocation. The dossier has no citable record covering the report's date line, so the report date is stated here and is not entered as a later event. Prior research: the packet reports no prior claim or review for this order. Searches: current-law:usc, current-law:ecfr and later-events:ofr_catalog, each marked complete in the packet with no coverage warning; they are the packet's machine searches. Extension of the report deadline: the held National Mediation Board report copy (a held text lead, not an authenticated original, so it cannot be registered as a later-event source in this sheet) states that the President approved on February 19, 1949 an extension of the report time to not later than April 13, 1949; the extension moves the report deadline and is not an end, and it is disclosed here and not entered as a later event.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Earlier-end check: the order's own terms contain a thirty-day report deadline and a standstill running until thirty days after the report, neither of which is an end; no stated fixed end date or termination clause appears in the held text. The packet's bounded number search of 33,118 held texts (clause drafts excluded) found citations only in this order's own printing, the National Mediation Board report copy. The dossier's chain of later events names only Executive Order 12553 as acting on this order, and the three selected machine searches show no revoking, superseding or terminating instrument before 1986-02-25.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Independent commands: the creation of the board, the bar on interested members, the report deadline, the standstill all concern the single dispute the order addressed; none is a separate delegation, standing rule or continuing regulation. The standstill sentence restates section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, which continues on its own statutory force; the period it measures here was set by this order's creation of the board and nothing held shows it still running.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings: Executive Order 12553 contains no saving clause (the enacting sentence is followed only by the table). The order's one-time commands (creating the board, the report, the status quo period) were exhausted by performance or lapse, section 10 of the Railway Labor Act continues independently and needs no support from this order, and no surviving appointment, transfer or right is shown in any held source. State none_after_bounded_search.\",\"scope_check\":\"Scope: Executive Order 12553 revokes the order by number, the table entry matches the order's subject, and the enacting sentence carries no exception, so the revocation reaches the whole order and every clause inventoried here.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:10032#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:10032#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","state":"match"},"sha256":"945348afbba863ae7dc1fbe7df8759682fe1733962734a9255a1062c0561048c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","text_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"49c4d157b85005a24041379993faf400dc6ee9078f9f3d5f72f3e7fab7b2b3f6","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10032.txt","sha256":"49c4d157b85005a24041379993faf400dc6ee9078f9f3d5f72f3e7fab7b2b3f6","state":"match"},"sha256":"d932782219e3f18165b8b34a840324970cee8be32ab592cffd5aa05774209e6d","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10032.txt","text_sha256":"49c4d157b85005a24041379993faf400dc6ee9078f9f3d5f72f3e7fab7b2b3f6"}]}]}

S. C. 160), I hereby create a board of \nthree members, to be appointed by me, \nto investigate the said dispute. No \nmember of the said board shall be pe­\ncuniarily or otherwise interested in any \norganization of railway employees or any \ncarrier.\nThe board shall report its findings to \nthe President with respect to the said \ndispute within thirty days from the date \nof this order.\nAs provided by section 10 of the Rail­\nway Labor Act, as amended, from this \ndate and for thirty days after the board \nhas made its report to the President, no \nchange, except by agreement, shall be \nmade by the Union Railroad Company \n(Pittsburgh) orbits employees in the con­\nditions out of which the said dispute \narose."},"id":"NA:astra-pullman-10056-transmittal-v1","instrument_id":"EO:10056","legal_effect":"unresolved","producer":"astra-report-bridge-producer","question_key":"NQ:b5f93f3a50e883277a10687e7cfe5a41bb7424b8fee07e9752d646d7d3b63d34","schema":"norton-application/v1","scope_id":"EO:10056#whole","state":"candidate"},"application_review":null,"candidate_evidence_ids":[],"citable_factual_events":[],"factual_event_errors":[],"factual_review_status":"candidate","input_origin":"jobs/norton/output/FRAGMENTS.jsonl","legal_effect":"unresolved","machine_lead":true,"question":{"edition_required":"PEB-075 report historical transmittal letter (NMB-held scan)","entity_id":"Presidential Emergency Board 75 established by EO:10056 on 1949-05-12","event_kind":"other_event","interval_end":"1986-02-25","interval_start":"1949-05-12","object":"PEB 75 final report transmittal letter","origin":"owner_seeded","origin_reference":"Owner leverage-first campaign, 2026-10-22; report-deadline ruling 2026-10-21","proposition":"What date does the identified report transmittal letter bear? The order creates a three-member emergency board under section 10 of the Railway Labor Act to investigate the dispute between the carriers represented by the Eastern Carriers' Conference Committee, the Western Carriers' Conference Committee and the Southeastern Carriers' Conference Committee and certain of their employees represented by the Order of Railway Conductors and the Brotherhood of Railroad Trainmen, requires the board to report within thirty days from the date of the order. Inventory: the order is one operative unit; the creation of the board, its thirty-day report deadline and the status-quo sentence restating section 10 are all within the single whole-order clause confirmed here. Ending: Executive Order 12553 of February 25, 1986 (51 FR 7237, held government printing) revokes the numbered orders in its table, and any amendments thereto; its No. Subject table lists 10112 with a subject line matching this order (creating an emergency board to investigate a railroad labor dispute). The enacting provision has no stated effective date, so the revocation is read as effective on signing, 1986-02-25; the Federal Register publication date 1986-03-03 and the filing date are not effective dates. The thirty-day or stated report date and the end of any statutory status-quo period are deadlines and spent facts, not ends (owner ruling 2026-10-21 on report deadlines): the order stayed on the books until express revocation. Searches: the OFR executive-order catalog search and the USC and eCFR citation searches (coverage notes read; no coverage warning) show no instrument other than EO 12553 revoking, superseding or amending this order; the held-text citation search found citations of this number only in printings of the order itself, orders printed on the same page, Federal Register index or codification-guide material and (for some) National Mediation Board report front matter.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Candidate earlier ends examined. (1) The thirty-day report deadline is a deadline, not an end (owner ruling 2026-10-21): the order does not end when the board reports, nor when the statutory thirty-day status-quo period after the report runs out; actual report and standstill dates are spent facts that no reviewed event record in this packet supplies, so they are disclosed here rather than recorded as later events. (2) The statutory standstill in section 10 of the Railway Labor Act is the statute's own effect and not a separate command of this order. (3) No later order amends, supersedes or revokes the order before EO 12553 in the held corpus (OFR catalog and number-citation searches). EO 12553 is the first held instrument that revokes it. No earlier end is shown, so none is claimed.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Independent commands: the order has no command independent of the board's creation and reporting. The thirty-day report requirement and the status-quo sentence are parts of the same board-creating order and share its end; the status-quo sentence restates section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, whose force comes from that statute.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings and residual effects: EO 12553's enacting provision contains no savings or exception clause (the ACT record for this pair lists none, and the held text has none). The board's work, report and any agreement reached are completed past events rather than continuing commands of this order; the order created no right, appointment or transfer that survives independently of it, and no held source shows any continuing independent effect.\",\"scope_check\":\"Scope: EO 12553 revokes the order by number and the held table row matches its subject; the enacting provision reaches any amendments thereto and has no exception, so it reaches the whole order, not part. The order creates a three-member emergency board under section 10 of the Railway Labor Act to investigate the dispute between the Pullman Company and certain of its employees represented by the Order of Railway Conductors, requires the board to report within thirty days from the date of the order. Inventory: the order is one operative unit; the creation of the board, its thirty-day report deadline and the status-quo sentence restating section 10 are all within the single whole-order clause confirmed here. Ending: Executive Order 12553 of February 25, 1986 (51 FR 7237, held government printing) revokes the numbered orders in its table, and any amendments thereto; its No. Subject table lists 10140 with a subject line matching this order (creating an emergency board to investigate a railroad labor dispute). The enacting provision has no stated effective date, so the revocation is read as effective on signing, 1986-02-25; the Federal Register publication date 1986-03-03 and the filing date are not effective dates. The thirty-day or stated report date and the end of any statutory status-quo period are deadlines and spent facts, not ends (owner ruling 2026-10-21 on report deadlines): the order stayed on the books until express revocation. Searches: the OFR executive-order catalog search and the USC and eCFR citation searches (coverage notes read; no coverage warning) show no instrument other than EO 12553 revoking, superseding or amending this order; the held-text citation search found citations of this number only in printings of the order itself, orders printed on the same page, Federal Register index or codification-guide material and (for some) National Mediation Board report front matter.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Candidate earlier ends examined. (1) The thirty-day report deadline is a deadline, not an end (owner ruling 2026-10-21): the order does not end when the board reports, nor when the statutory thirty-day status-quo period after the report runs out; actual report and standstill dates are spent facts that no reviewed event record in this packet supplies, so they are disclosed here rather than recorded as later events. (2) The statutory standstill in section 10 of the Railway Labor Act is the statute's own effect and not a separate command of this order. (3) No later order amends, supersedes or revokes the order before EO 12553 in the held corpus (OFR catalog and number-citation searches). EO 12553 is the first held instrument that revokes it. No earlier end is shown, so none is claimed.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Independent commands: the order has no command independent of the board's creation and reporting. The thirty-day report requirement and the status-quo sentence are parts of the same board-creating order and share its end; the status-quo sentence restates section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, whose force comes from that statute.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings and residual effects: EO 12553's enacting provision contains no savings or exception clause (the ACT record for this pair lists none, and the held text has none). The board's work, report and any agreement reached are completed past events rather than continuing commands of this order; the order created no right, appointment or transfer that survives independently of it, and no held source shows any continuing independent effect.\",\"scope_check\":\"Scope: EO 12553 revokes the order by number and the held table row matches its subject; the enacting provision reaches any amendments thereto and has no exception, so it reaches the whole order, not part. I read the whole order; the representation is the machine-extracted text layer of the official PDF (OCR for this period), and I did not inspect the page images. End: express revocation by Executive Order 12553 on 1986-02-25, under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on report deadlines (option C): a report deadline and a spent standstill are not ends. Searches: current-law:usc, current-law:ecfr and later-events:ofr_catalog, each marked complete in the dossier; the U.S. Code and eCFR searches found no hit naming this order. The held-text number search for this order found no held later order that cites it other than Executive Order 12553.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"The order contains no fixed end date and no termination clause. Its commands are a bar on interested board members, a requirement that the board report within thirty days from the date of the order (due by April 17, 1960), and a standstill running from the date of the order until thirty days after the report. The report deadline and the standstill are spent facts, not ends, under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on report deadlines, and neither is claimed as the end. A held lead copy of the report of Emergency Board No. 128 (a text of the front matter of the National Mediation Board publication, graded held_text_lead, a grade the claim format does not accept as a cited source) is dated June 2, 1960; the board report states that the board was granted an extension of time to report to June 2, 1960. Thirty days after June 2, 1960 is July 2, 1960. Those dates come from a lead and are disclosed, not claimed as events or as the end: the clause is described only as functionally spent on June 2, 1960, the report date, with the standstill functionally spent on July 2, 1960. My reading of the held texts of Executive Order 12553 and the number search of the held texts show no earlier revoking, superseding or terminating instrument for this order.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Each command (create the board, bar interested members, require the report, freeze conditions) depends on this order and on the dispute it created; none is a separate delegation, continuing regulation or standing rule. The standstill sentence applies section 10 of the Railway Labor Act (45 U.S.C. 160); the statute is a separate source of law that an Executive order does not revoke, but the periods measured here were set by this order's creation of the board, and no held claim-grade source shows them still running when the order was revoked.\",\"savings_check\":\"Executive Order 12553 was read in full: it consists of one revoking sentence, a table of the revoked orders by number and subject, an editorial note and the signature block, and it contains no saving or transition provision. No residual effect of this order is shown to depend on its continued force. Section 10 of the Railway Labor Act continues independently as stated in the independent-command check.\",\"scope_check\":\"One clause is inventoried: the operative text from the order heading through the standstill sentence (the recitals, the creation of the board of three members, the bar on interested members, the report requirement and the standstill). The signature, the date line and any attached lists follow it; the attached lists, where present, only identify carriers and labor organizations and contain no command. Executive Order 12553 revokes the order by number as a whole, not in part, so every command in the clause ends on the same date. Preparation attestations never approve legal conclusions or replace whole-case review.
